# Sentence-Shaped JSON Keys Audit

Generated by enumeration only. I did not edit any `ssot` object.

## Scope

- Canonical corpus used: direct files matching `ssot/<topic>/<topic>.json`: 155 files.
- Excluded direct noncanonical `ssot/*/*.json` file(s): 1 (`ssot/arni-hfref/manuscript_docmodel.json`).
- Nested `sources/` and `appraisal/` JSON files were outside the requested `ssot/*/*.json` canonical-object scope.

## Rule

I treated a key as sentence-shaped if, after splitting on ASCII alphabetic word tokens:

1. it had at least 7 alphabetic tokens; and
2. either it contained a space or colon and at least one verb/function token, or it was underscore/hyphen shaped and contained at least one verb-like token plus either a function token or an enumerated prefix `a_` through `g_`.

The verb/function lists were fixed before classification and included auxiliaries such as `is/are/was/does/has/can/should`, action words such as `pooled/registered/checked/withdrawn/corrected`, and function words such as `the/and/not/no/because/where/why/what/which/that`. This intentionally catches long prose clauses such as `THE_COMPARATORS_ARE_THREE_DIFFERENT_DRUGS` while leaving ordinary shorter schema names such as `risk_of_bias_overall` outside the boundary.

## Counts

- Distinct key names seen: 3949
- Distinct key names captured by the rule: 228
- Distinct key names not captured: 3721
- Captured key occurrences: 648
- Non-captured key occurrences: 101081
- Classification counts: ACTED ON 114, RECORDED ONLY 7, COULD NOT TELL 107

## Static-Vs-Dynamic Disclosure

| Item | Static or dynamic | Disclosure |
|---|---:|---|
| Sentence-key rule | Static | Token threshold and token lists are fixed in this report. |
| File list, keys, paths, values, counts | Dynamic | Parsed from the 155 canonical JSON files on disk. |
| ACTED/RECORDED/UNKNOWN classes | Mixed | High-risk keys use manual evidence from object fields, renderer code, and HTML spot checks; remaining keys use conservative path/value heuristics and are often `COULD NOT TELL`. |
| Corrections | Static | None made. This report is enumeration only. |

## RECORDED ONLY Ranked

1. `estimand_established_does_not_cover_the_contrast_2026_08_20` (156 occurrence(s), 125 object(s))
   - Unacted-on defect: The corpus-wide caveat says `estimand_established` validates shared measurement but not the randomized/concurrent comparator contrast; in most delivered pages that caveat is not shown where the flag or estimate is shown.
   - Reader currently shown instead: A reader generally sees the live result/estimand fields without this caveat; an HTML search found the exact key/wording rendered only in ATTR_PN_REVIEW.html, where ATTR-PN was separately withdrawn.
   - Top-three justification: It is corpus-wide: 156 outcome blocks across 125 objects can let a reader overread an estimand flag as contrast validation.

2. `AND_THIS_TRIAL_HAS_TWO_CO_PRIMARIES` (1 occurrence(s), 1 object(s))
   - Unacted-on defect: DECLARE-TIMI 58 has another same-rank co-primary, but the SGLT2 MACE review used the MACE composite without a recorded selection decision in that registration-read field.
   - Reader currently shown instead: SGLT2_MACE_CVOT_REVIEW.html still shows the pooled OR 0.9074 (0.831 to 0.9908); later page prose says the pool is referred and the estimate is unchanged.
   - Top-three justification: The live estimate excludes no effect and the unmade selection among co-primaries changes what question the page appears to answer.

3. `why_it_is_referred_rather_than_withdrawn_here` (1 occurrence(s), 1 object(s))
   - Unacted-on defect: The rosuvastatin pooled estimate is referred because it averages mismatched composites, but withdrawal/restatement was explicitly deferred.
   - Reader currently shown instead: ROSUVASTATIN_AUTO_FULL_REVIEW.html still shows OR 0.6561 (0.484 to 0.8894), with prose saying the pool is referred, not withdrawn.
   - Top-three justification: A significant live estimate remains on a pool the object says has the same estimand-mixing shape that caused another pool to be withdrawn.

4. `a_a_published_synthesis_carried_more_trials_than_this_pool` (4 occurrence(s), 4 object(s))
   - Unacted-on defect: A published synthesis carried more trials than the object pool, but the object does not widen the search or re-pool; it records a scope gap.
   - Reader currently shown instead: Where live, the reader is shown the smaller object pool plus a note that the larger evidence base exists and a search-widening decision has not been made.

5. `a_a_published_review_pools_six_trials_where_this_pools_two` (1 occurrence(s), 1 object(s))
   - Unacted-on defect: The nirsevimab object carries two trials while a published review carried six; the missing four are not identified and no widened synthesis is made.
   - Reader currently shown instead: NIRSEVIMAB_INFANT_RSV_REVIEW.html shows RR 0.2605 (0.1766 to 0.3845), with an alert that the published review had six trials.

6. `b_whether_the_extra_trials_can_be_named` (4 occurrence(s), 4 object(s))
   - Unacted-on defect: For several larger-literature comparisons, the object records that extra trials exist but does not identify them from an included-study table.
   - Reader currently shown instead: The reader is shown the current pool and a count-only caveat rather than a reconciled included-trial list.

7. `b_which_four_are_missing_is_not_established` (1 occurrence(s), 1 object(s))
   - Unacted-on defect: The nirsevimab object knows four trials are absent from the object pool but has not established which four.
   - Reader currently shown instead: The reader is shown the two-trial RR 0.2605 and a caveat that the absent four are counted but unidentified.
